<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which description best captures how this protein’s magnesium transport responds to cytoplasmic magnesium levels?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> High cytoplasmic magnesium activates magnesium influx.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Low cytoplasmic magnesium activates magnesium efflux.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Low cytoplasmic magnesium activates magnesium influx.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Magnesium influx proceeds constitutively, independent of cytoplasmic magnesium levels.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Analyzing the protein sequence, I can identify several key features:<br>- Multiple hydrophobic stretches indicating transmembrane domains<br>- An N-terminal region with mixed acidic/basic residues<br>- A prominent polybasic C-terminal region (KKKKKPRKRRTL)<br><br>This protein is characteristic of bacterial magnesium transporters, specifically members of the CorA family (or related families like MgtE). These are well-studied integral membrane proteins that function as magnesium influx channels.<br><br>The regulatory mechanism of these magnesium transporters is well-characterized:<br>- They contain a cytoplasmic sensing domain that monitors intracellular Mg²⁺ concentration<br>- When cytoplasmic magnesium levels are HIGH, the transporter is inhibited/closed (feedback inhibition)<br>- When cytoplasmic magnesium levels are LOW, the transporter opens to allow magnesium INFLUX to replenish cellular magnesium stores<br><br>This is the biologically logical response — the cell imports more magnesium when its internal concentration drops below the required threshold. - Having transmembrane domains
- Being sensitive to cytoplasmic magnesium levels
- Being inhibited by high cytoplasmic magnesium
- Being activated when cytoplasmic magnesium is low

The CorA family of magnesium transporters:
- They are pentameric channels
- They transport Mg2+ into the cell (influx)
- They are gated by cytoplasmic Mg2+ - high Mg2+ inhibits transport (negative feedback)
- When cytoplasmic Mg2+ is low, the channel opens and Mg2+ flows in (influx)
- They can also catalyze efflux under certain conditions, but their primary function is influx

The mechanism: The cytoplasmic domain of CorA has a binding site for Mg2+. When Mg2+ binds (high cytoplasmic Mg2+), it induces a conformational change that closes the channel, preventing further influx. When Mg2+ levels are low, the cytoplasmic domain is unbound, allowing the channel to remain open, and Mg2+ flows down its electrochemical gradient into the cell.

So the answer should be: Low cytoplasmic magnesium activates magnesium influx (C).

This makes biological sense - the cell needs to bring in more Mg2+ when levels are low, and stop influx when levels are sufficient.
